- **Photo-viewer wiring reused verbatim, nothing new invented:** the fixed sections' `open()`/`data-ren-tl-open` tiles are the same mechanism the TOP tiles already use (generalised with `data-ren-tl-shot="1"` for both sub and flash — the boolean "this reference is shot-scoped" flag already used for sublingual); `data-ren-cap-shot` on the Add button carries the real shot key (`"sublingual"`/`"flash"`) the capture flow already reads generically elsewhere. The viewer's own row-scoped arrow-stepping (`phRenViewSetFor`, keyed by `phRenRowKeyOf`) already walks `tongue:flash`/`tongue:sublingual` as their own rows — no change needed there, confirmed by reading it rather than assumed.
- **CSS**, new and narrowly scoped: `.ph-tc-sec` (2-column grid: photos + chips, no Past-photos column — the fixed sections have no picker to put one beside), `.ph-tc-sec-photos`, `.ph-tc-sec-chips`, `.ph-tc-flashnote`, plus a one-line addition to the existing 980px breakpoint (`.ph-tc-sec` stacks to 1 column). Everything else (`.ph-tc-tile`/`.ph-tc-hd`/`.ph-tc-open`/`.ph-tc-empty`/`.ph-tng2-seg`/`.ph-tng2-row`) is reused as-is.
- **Summary, Fang Bing Ren count and Clear stay in the TOP panel only, unconditionally** (not kind-gated) — they reflect the whole tongue finding (colour/shape/coat/under/marks together), not one chip category, so they'd read the same regardless of which kind-specific chips are showing; splitting them per kind would have meant three copies of the same "Reads as" sentence for no reason.
- Verified live via a scratch local server + the Browser pane, synthetic photo records injected straight into `phRenPhotoCache` (today natural/sublingual/flash, a week-ago natural/sublingual — no flash a week ago, deliberately, to exercise the "no earlier photo" empty state) and real calls to `phTngCmpPick`/`phTngCmpHtml` (not a hand-typed stand-in): `phTngCmpPick(name,"t",...)` correctly excludes flash and sublingual from both today's and last's picks; `"sub"` and `"flash"` each correctly isolate their own shot; the flash kind's `last` correctly comes back `null` and its fixed section renders "No earlier photo on file" + the disclosed note; the TOP panel's rendered HTML contains no "Under" text under `kind:"t"` and no "Tongue body"/More-fold button under `kind:"sub"`, while Under IS present under `"sub"`; exactly two `.ph-tc-sec` blocks render (Sublingual, Flash) with real, non-overlapping `getBoundingClientRect()`s (845×327 each) once injected into the real `#pharmacyPage` DOM with the real stylesheet applied. Full page boot showed no new console errors (the one present is the known offline-sandbox external-script-fetch limitation, unrelated).
- **Not exercised:** a real patient, her phone, a real tap through the rendered UI (only direct function calls and DOM injection were tested — no click was dispatched through the real `data-tcmp-kind`/`data-ren-tl-open`/`data-visit-tng2` handlers), an actual pixel screenshot (the sandbox's hidden-pane/sign-in-overlay limitation this file already documents — computed-style and `getBoundingClientRect()` verification was used instead).

## IVF history redesign: the full 6-point rebuild (her 2026-10-16, mock `ivf-redesign-mock-2026-10-16.html`, "Yes, build all of it"; six commits on `session-a` — all LIVE on main (confirmed ancestors of origin/main as of the 2026-10-18 audit))
A full UX redesign of IVF-history recording, mocked and approved whole, then built in six passes (the commit messages number them 1/6-3/6 against the ORIGINAL plan order below, which does not line up 1:1 with the numbering inside each message — read this entry, not the numbers in `git log`, for which commit is which point).
1. **The round card stays open once opened** (`rec.ivfHistOpen`/`rec.ivfOpenIds`), persisting across visits — commit `fbcef68`.
2. **One merged outcome field** (replacing two previously unsynced mechanisms) — commit `fbcef68`.
3. **Starting a transfer shows/lets her change which round it attaches to** (ambiguous-pick flagging + one-tap reattachment) — commit `fbcef68`.
4. **Quick-log by moment** (`fbcef68`'s sibling, commit `f8b0e41`): four single-purpose quick forms (Scan check-in / Retrieval day / Transfer day / Results call) on the IVF history card instead of the full round-card funnel, today's due moment auto-detected and highlighted (`phIvfQuickDue`, priority order results > transfer > retrieval > scan). Each writes the exact same round/transfer/scan shape the existing "+ Egg collection"/"+ Transfer" doors use — a faster door onto the one record, never a second one. Added an honest new `folTotal` field (a combined follicle count, never folded into the one-sided `folR`, which would misreport it) threaded through every scan display and write path.
5. **Scan bloods merged into the shared Blood-tests store** (commit `1dfa932`): an IVF monitoring scan's e2/lh/p4/fsh/amh are the same markers `PH_BLD_MARKERS` and the Natural-fertility Timeline's own "Day 2-3 hormones"/AMH items already track. `phBldList` now merges them in on every READ (`phIvfScanBldRows`) rather than copying them into a second stored record, so editing the scan stays the one edit. hCG was deliberately left out — no catalog entry, rare on a monitoring scan, and a pregnancy hCG already has its own correct home on the transfer (`t.pregHcg`). The Blood-tests table shows a merged column read-only (no edit/delete there — the scan itself is the one place to change it); typical-range flags (none fire — cycle hormones carry no fixed range) and the 12-month staleness rule both apply normally. Verified this makes the Timeline's AMH/Day-2-3-hormones items resolve from real scan data with no separate entry, and that it doesn't touch Fang Bing Ren's existing blood ticks (uric acid, etc. — none of the merged markers are wired to a sign).
6. **A cross-patient "today's IVF work" list** (commit `67e2535`): **disclosed finding, not a silent reinterpretation** — the plan's own wording ("behind Appointments' existing 'Cycle & IVF' lens chip") assumed a UI entry point that turned out to be stale: `phApptLensHit`'s `"cycle"` branch is still live code, but the lens-chip ROW it belonged to was superseded by later redesigns (the Appointments List rebuild, the 2026-10-15 dead-code sweep removing `PH_APPTCAL_LENSES`) and nothing in the current app still sets `phApptCalLens` to `"cycle"`/`"herbs"`/`"new"`/`"home"` — only `"all"` and `"prac:<name>"` (the practitioner row) remain reachable. Rather than resurrect a chip for a DIFFERENT, broader filter ("on a cycle-tracking plan" vs. "has IVF action due today"), this builds the actual deliverable directly: a "Today's IVF work" band on the Appointments page (`phIvfTodayWorkBandHtml`, right under the practitioner chip row) scanning EVERY patient on file (`phIvfTodayWorkRows`, not just today's bookings — monitoring often happens at the fertility clinic with no LCM appointment that day) through `phIvfQuickDue`, so this list and the quick-log bar can never disagree about what's due. No fold, no new page — hidden entirely when nothing's due anywhere, same posture as the existing "Herbs to prepare today" list.
- Every point sandbox-verified through real DOM click/change-event dispatch (not direct function calls) against synthetic patients, deleted after each verification pass: all four quick-log moments write their exact fields; the due-priority order resolves correctly when several moments could apply at once; the "no round yet"/"no transfer awaiting" fallback messages render; the quick bar and the IVF-today band both stay hidden for a patient/practice with nothing due; the merged blood row renders correctly alongside a real manual blood test with correct sort/flagging/staleness; the Appointments band's patient-name link opens the real profile; no new console errors anywhere.
- **Git housekeeping, disclosed:** built across two sessions on one shared working tree, each commit isolated to its own hunks via `git apply --cached` + a no-paths `git commit` (never `git add -A`/`git commit -- <paths>`) specifically because a concurrent session was mid-edit on `index.html` at the same time — confirmed before every commit that nothing of theirs was swept in. Both version markers (`sw.js` `CACHE_VERSION`, the `<meta name="lcm-build">` stamp) were bumped together on every one of the six commits.
- **Not exercised anywhere in this build:** a real patient, her phone, a real screenshot (the sandbox's sign-in overlay covers the DOM even though the real render and every event dispatch work correctly through direct access — a known, previously-documented limitation of this environment).

## Patient details: the Came in for / Treatment Care Plan one-box card is wider (her 2026-10-16 screenshot + "extend the width of box", build 20261016-1017000, committed on `session-a`)
The right column of Patient details' two-up record (`.ph-pf-rec2`) carries the Identity card fixed at 316px on the left and the Came in for / Treatment Care Plan one-box card (`.ph-pf-onebox`) in the remaining space on the right — that remaining space was capped by the row's own `max-width: 976px` (set 2026-10-10, "too wide, too much empty space", a deliberate cap at the time). ## Find the Ren: no more side box, chips grouped and ordered, Abdominal demoted, flat list chips smaller (her 2026-10-16 design review of two phone screenshots, four linked asks on one component, treated as one pass; committed on `session-a` — LIVE on main (audited 2026-10-18: origin/main has since advanced past this build))
Her four asks: (1) the LOOK card's colour side box — "i dont like the side box with that colour, the ui design takes up too much space ... remove and prevent this table style. Group the chips together e.g. face: colour and texture"; (2) the flat BUILD/SKIN/COMPLEXION selected-signs list — "make sub categories of build skin complexion so abdominal doesnt precede all signs symptoms"; (3) confirmed via Q&A — "Build: lightest to darkest" is a weight spectrum (thin/delicate → slim → slightly heavy → full-figured → robust/obesity), Skin/Face colour goes light→dark then texture after; (4) "find a better way to make chips smaller to fit more on page" on the flat list.
- **One shared ordering table** (`PH_RQ_SUBJ_ORDER`, `PH_RQ_SUBGROUPS`, `PH_RQ_ORDER_IX`, `phSignCmpSubjOrder`, just above `PH_REN_GROUPS`): Claude-drafted subgroups per subject — Build: Weight (light→heavy spectrum) + Shape; Complexion/Skin: Colour (light→dark) + Texture; Lips: Colour + Condition; Eyes: Look + Vision; Muscles: Tone + Symptom — hers to correct. One table, two readers: `phRqRows` (the Look card) and `phFbrGroupedHtml`'s new optional `sortSubj` field on a group (the flat list) — her "one mechanism" rule, not two.
- **LOOK card** (`phRqRows`, `phRqCardHtml`): each row's signs now also carry `groups` (subgrouped + ordered) alongside the existing flat `signs` (same shape as before, so `phRqRowDone`, the "Looked:" line and the kbd numbering all keep working unchanged). The chip markup adds a quiet inline `.ph-rq-glab` label ("Weight"/"Colour"/...) right before its own chip cluster, in the SAME wrapping row as the chips — no new line, no new box. `data-rq-look`/`data-rq-none`/`data-rq-next` and the click handler (`phRqClick`) are untouched.
- **Side box removed, Find the Ren only**: `.ph-rq .ph-pfw-q.done` loses its teal box-shadow rail AND its settle-flash animation; `.ph-rq .ph-pfw-q.cur` loses the gold ring, replaced by a plain `--ph-herb-tint` background (colour and space, not a box, per her own separation rule). Scoped to `.ph-rq` — every other screen reusing the shared `.ph-pfw-q` classes (Usual period, Cycle day log, Discharge, ...) keeps its rail/ring exactly as before. The "done" row needed no replacement mark: the ticked chip already fills solid teal.
- **Less vertical space on mobile**: inside the ONE existing 640px PHONE REDESIGN block (no new media query), the LOOK row's label column narrows 78px→56px and its padding tightens, so the chips wrap into more usable width on a ~360px phone.
- **Flat list, "Build, skin & complexion"**: the group object gained `sortSubj: PH_RQ_SUBJ_ORDER`; `phFbrGroupedHtml` sorts a group by that order (falling back to the old `phSignCmp` alphabetical sort) only when a group sets it — every other group/caller (`PH_BING_GROUPS`, the Treatment table's `phTsSubjRows`, etc.) behaves exactly as before. Live-verified sequence: Build → Skin → Complexion → Lips → Eyes → Muscles → Abdominal → (Age/Constitution/Mouth & throat/Sex/Sweat, alphabetical, as before) — Abdominal no longer opens the list.
- **Chip density, the flat list** (`.ph-fbr-row`/`.ph-fbr-main`/`.ph-fbr-bar`/`.ph-fbr-ck`/`.ph-fbr-x`): padding and the two round controls shrink a step (`.ph-fbr-main` 7px→4px vertical padding, the bar 22px→18px, the check badge 18px→16px, the ✕ 22px→18px) — all on or near the spacing scale, the 44px floor already retired. **Disclosed, not scoped to Find the Ren**: `phFbrRowHtml`/`.ph-fbr-row` is the one shared sign-row primitive, reused by the Fang Bing Ren board, the Ren tab's own lists and Quick assess's evidence rows too — a smaller row only under Find the Ren would make one screen's rows a different size to the identical-looking rows one scroll away, exactly the "one page only" inconsistency her own conventions flag as a bug. The LOOK card's own `.ph-pfw-opt` pills (already the 28px TAPPABLE THINGS chip) were left alone, as asked.
- Verified live in the sandbox (scratch server, Browser pane, a synthetic patient, real function calls — `phRqRows`, `phRqCardHtml`, `phRenTabHtml`, `phTriWrite`/`phTriSetYes`/`phTriSetNo` — never hand-typed stand-ins): Build/Complexion/Skin/Lips/Eyes/Muscles rows group and order exactly as designed (read back from the real returned `groups`); the rendered card's kbd numbers run 1-9 continuously across subgroups; a real tick fills the chip solid teal and, once a whole row is answered, its computed `box-shadow` and `animation` both read `none`, confirming the side box and flash are gone; the real flat list's Build/Skin blocks render in the new subject order with the new per-subject key order; at 375px the LOOK row's grid narrows to `56px ...` with no horizontal overflow; at 1280px the desktop 78px column and the un-ringed `.cur` tint both hold; no new console errors at either width. Synthetic patient deleted after.
- **Not exercised:** a real patient, her phone, a real tap through the delegated click listener (the sandbox has no signed-in patient panel open for it to resolve a patient from — the same documented limitation as several earlier entries in this file; `phTriSetYes`/`phTriSetNo` were called directly instead, the exact write path `phRqClick` itself calls), a real screenshot.
- **Open question for her:** the flat list fix reorders Abdominal (and the group's other stray subjects — Sweat, Mouth & throat, Constitution, Sex, Age) after the six named ones, rather than giving each its own labelled subheading inside the group. If she'd rather see small "BUILD / SKIN / COMPLEXION / ABDOMINAL" subheadings there (closer to the Fang Bing Ren board's own area style) than rely on each row's own bold subject prefix to read as the grouping, that's a quick follow-up, not a rebuild.

## Tongue photo viewer: multi-select Colour, Purple/Blue, centre crack (her synth22 batch, Workstream B; committed on `session-a` — LIVE on main (audited 2026-10-18: origin/main has since advanced past this build))
Scope = the single-photo Tongue viewer's analysis rows only (`phRenTngSecHtml` + the shared `PH_TNG_*` constants/`phTng2*` functions it and the standalone Examine page both read). Did NOT touch the Find the Ren work (`3d36675`, a sibling build) or `phRqCardHtml`/`PH_RQ_LOOK`/`PH_REN_GROUPS`.
- **"Improve UI" (her ask 1):** no further visual redesign was made beyond what asks 2-4 needed. This screen already got a dedicated polish pass the previous day (the 2026-10-15 "chip audit — joined segmented bars, grouped" build + the 2026-10-16 "Sublingual and Flash as their own fixed sections" build) — the new multi-select Colour chips inherit that same joined-bar look automatically (`seg()` wasn't touched), and a FURTHER unprompted redesign on top of that would need a fresh mock + her sign-off under her own "never push significant visual changes live unreviewed" rule, which is out of scope for an unattended batch pass. Flag to her if she meant something more specific.
- **Multi-select, Colour and Shape (her ask 2):** **Shape was already multi-select** (`t.shape` is an array via `phTng2Toggle`'s existing branch) — nothing to change there; her screenshot likely read it as single-pick only because a joined segmented bar looks the same whether it holds one pick or several (that's the point of the 2026-10-15 bar rule). **Colour was genuinely single-pick**, now widened: `tng.colour` stays the PRIMARY value — the exact same bare string every existing reader already expects (the chart SVG body colour, the "Reads as" summary, the two Fang Bing Ren chart-word readers `phFbrTng2Words` and `phPatClues`' own tongue flag reader) — and a new `tng.colourMore[]` holds any FURTHER colours ticked. This is the Pulse chart's own "several qualities in one cell" `base + more[]` pattern (her 2026-10-13 build), reused rather than the `coatQual`/`marks` keyed-object shape, because it lets every existing single-string reader keep working untouched instead of needing a rewrite. `phTng2Toggle`'s new `colour` branch: tapping an unticked chip sets it as primary if none is set, else pushes it onto `colourMore`; tapping the primary again untick it and promotes the next `colourMore` entry (if any); tapping a ticked secondary removes just that one. `phTng2Read` normalises `colourMore` to an array and de-dupes/strips anything matching the primary (defensive, in case of a bad write). `phTng2Empty` and `phTng2Summary` (the "Reads as" sentence) both read `colourMore` too. **Old saved data (a bare string, no `colourMore` key) reads correctly with no migration** — `Object.assign` over `phTng2Blank()`'s new `colourMore: []` default handles it. Changed in the shared `phTng2*` functions so the standalone Examine page (`phVisitTongueHtml`'s `colourRow`) picks up the same multi-select behaviour automatically (her own "both screens should behave consistently" allowance) — its layout was not touched, only the "on" check for the colour chip (`t.colour === k || t.colourMore.includes(k)`), same one-line fix as the viewer's own `colour` row. **Coat thickness/colour deliberately left single-pick** — a disclosed, smaller-surface scope cut (the task's own fallback), since Coat wasn't asked about as explicitly as Colour/Shape and widening it would mean auditing a second set of readers for no confirmed need yet.
- **Purple and Blue (her ask 3):** **"Purple" already existed** in `PH_TNG_COLOURS` since the original detailed-tongue-chart build (`8f7e177`) — it was not missing, and was already wired to the Ren sign `tongue_purple` ("Tongue: dark or purple") via `PH_TNG_FBR_MAP` (`tongue_purple: [["colour","purple"]]`) and `PH_FBR_CHART_PREFILL` (`words: ["purple","dusky"]`) — those were simply dormant pending nothing, since the chip itself was already rendering on this exact screen before this session started. Nothing needed building for purple beyond what the multi-select change above already gives it (it can now sit alongside another ticked colour). **"Blue" is new** — added to `PH_TNG_COLOURS` with its own swatch hex (`#5C7CBB`), picked up automatically by every reader that was widened for multi-select above (chip row, chart-word extraction, summary). **Left OFF the Fang Bing Ren wiring on purpose**: the existing `tongue_purple` sign's own wording says "dark or purple," not "blue" — tying a blue tick to it would be inventing a clinical association she hasn't confirmed, not reusing an existing one (per the task's own instruction to disclose rather than invent). Disclosed gap, hers to extend if she wants a blue-tongue sign built.
- **Centre crack (her ask 4):** added to the shared `PH_TNG_MARKS` table (`["crackC", "centre crack"]`, between `crackH` and `teeth`) — both consumers that read this one array pick it up with no separate wiring: the chip row (`phRenTngSecHtml`, `phVisitTongueHtml`, the dated-review/template-findings editors) and the typed-circle tool's type picker (`phRenMarkTypePlaceHtml`). Follows the exact same pattern as `crack`/`crackH`, which also carry no Fang Bing Ren sign — so centre crack ticks nothing there either, consistent rather than a new precedent. Also added a small SVG drawing for it (`crackC`, a single fixed line down the tongue's centre, same "fixed drawing, not per-region" shape as `twolines`) so the optional "Drawing" toggle view stays visually complete — this one extra touch wasn't strictly asked for but costs one line and avoids a silently-missing glyph next to its siblings.
- **Verified:** a standalone Node-free logic harness (the real `phTng2Blank`/`Toggle`/`Read`/`Empty`/`Summary`/`phFbrTng2Words` bodies, copied verbatim and run inside headless Edge via `--dump-dom`, since this folder's 5-dev-server cap was already held by other concurrent chats and `preview_start` refused a 6th) confirmed, all passing: (a) an old bare-string record (`{colour:"red", shape:["swollen"]}`) still reads as `colour:"red"`, is not reported empty, and its summary is unchanged; (b) ticking a 2nd and 3rd colour builds `colourMore` correctly, untick-the-primary promotes the next tick, untick-the-last clears everything back to a genuinely empty record; (c) the summary sentence lists every ticked colour ("pale, blue, swollen"); (d) `phFbrTng2Words` emits BOTH the primary and a secondary colour, so the dormant `tongue_purple` prefill still fires even when purple is ticked as someone's *second* colour, not their first; (e) `blue` and `crackC`/"centre crack" exist as distinct, correctly-labelled entries, and shape's pre-existing multi-select is unaffected. Confirmed by direct code reading (not live-rendered) that `PH_TNG_MARKS` is the one array both the chip row and `phRenMarkTypePlaceHtml` consume, so centre crack reaches both automatically. **Not exercised: a live signed-in render of the viewer itself (chip markup, 1280/375px layout, a real click through the delegated handler, console-error sweep)** — this folder's dev-server slots were full from other chats' sessions throughout, and loading `index.html` directly via `file://` in headless Edge crashed ("Multiple targets are not supported in headless mode"), a known Chromium headless limitation this file hasn't hit before; recommend a quick visual check on her own machine or a later session with a free server slot before pushing. Also not exercised: her phone, a real tap on the circle tool's new "centre crack" type option.

## Pulse chart: a "Normal range" toggle (synth22 batch review, Workstream C — "show pulse changes overlapped. Give a toggle of normal pulse readings"; committed on `session-a` — LIVE on main (audited 2026-10-18: origin/main has since advanced past this build))
Scope = the Pulse exam page only (`phHxConstitPulseHtml`, the one live render of the organ-zone grid + Depth/Diameter/Amplitude score line + Shen-Hammer exam — confirmed by grep that `phVisitPulseScoresHtml`/`phVisitPulseHalfHtml` each have exactly one caller; the old per-visit wrapper `phVisitPulseHtml` was already dead code per the 2026-10-13 pulse multi-select note, only its handlers still run). Part one of her ask (the dashed prior-visit overlay) was already built — untouched here. Did NOT touch Find the Ren (`3d36675`) or the Tongue photo viewer work (`77b08c7`).
- **Investigated first, per the brief.** The per-cell "Normal" tick (thick-arch drawing) is a different thing, confirmed by reading the 2026-10-13 pulse-multi-select note — left alone. Searched the file for an existing numeric pulse-normal: `PH_PULSE_KB_SEED` (the read-only SHPD knowledge base) holds exactly ONE, its `syl-15` row — "Age-appropriate resting rate (Dr Shen): Birth–4y: 84–90/min · 10–15y: 78–80 · 16–40y: 72–78 · 40–50y: 72 · 50y+: 66–72. Women and children tend more Rapid than men; athletes generally Slower" — sourced from her own Bilton SHPD Foundations Class syllabus, already transcribed into the codebase, not invented here. The only other numeric precedent is `phPulseRestRapid`'s documented `>90` rapid cutoff (integrate33 batch 10, Huang Qin Tang), reading the first of begin/other/end (never exertion). Both reused verbatim — **no new clinical numbers invented**, so nothing needed a DRAFT flag.
- **Built:** `PH_PULSE_NORMAL_RATE` (the five `syl-15` age bands, copied as data) + `phPulseNormalBandFor(rec)` (reads `rec.dob` via the existing `phAgeOf`), `phPulseFirstRestRate(h)` (the exact same begin/other/end read `phPulseRestRapid` uses, so the two can never disagree about "today's resting rate"), `phPulseNormalRefText(rec, h)` (the quiet sentence: her age's own band if her DOB is on file and falls in one of the five explicit bands, else the full table; plus a quiet "today's resting rate is N — above/within the typical range" tail when a rate has been typed in the Shen-Hammer exam), and `phPulseNormalToggleHtml(rec, h)` (the chip + text). Pronoun-safe throughout (`phProRec`, no hand-typed she/her, per the standing 2026-10-14 pronoun rule).
- **Placement:** right after the Depth/Diameter/Amplitude score line (`phVisitPulseScoresHtml`), before the copy-to-clipboard note box — the suggested primary placement, and the only one with an honest number to show (the organ-zone Depth/Diameter/Amplitude scores are her own 0–1 composite index with no established "normal" anywhere in the codebase; inventing a reference band for THAT would have been exactly the fabrication the brief warned against, so the toggle surfaces the one real numeric reference — resting rate — instead).
- **Chip + persistence:** a TAPPABLE THINGS chip (`.ph-pulse-normal-tgl`, pill, 28px, 1px `--ph-line` outline, white; picked = solid `--ph-herb`, white text) — not a checkbox, not a link. Default **OFF**. View state only: `presHxConstitPulseNormalOn` (a plain in-memory module var), matching this exact screen's own other toggles (`presHxConstitPulseStamp`, `presHxConstitHmOpen` on the lines right above it) — never `localStorage` (that's the Tongue viewer's own, different pattern; this screen never uses it), never saved to the record. The click handler (`data-constit-pulse-normal-toggle`) sits beside the existing `data-constit-pulse-copy` handler and just flips the var + `renderHxScreen()`, no save, no animation (nothing to guard under reduced motion).
- **Render function stays pure:** `phPulseNormalToggleHtml`/`phPulseNormalBandFor`/`phPulseNormalRefText` only read `rec.dob` and the passed-in hammer object — no mutation, consistent with the standing "render from `phPatientPeek`, never `phPatientRec`" rule (this function already receives whichever `rec` its caller passes, same as the rest of `phHxConstitPulseHtml`).
- **Verified** (`preview_start` refused a 6th dev server for this folder, same 5-slot cap the two sibling sessions hit; fell back to the standalone-harness technique — the real new functions pasted verbatim into a scratch HTML file, run through headless Edge via PowerShell `Start-Process ... --dump-dom` / `--screenshot`, same pattern CLAUDE.md's "Headless Edge screenshots" note describes): toggle defaults OFF (screenshot: plain outlined pill, no text) and renders ON correctly (screenshot: solid teal pill + the reference sentence); age-band selection correct for 37F ("72–78/min") and 76M ("66–72/min", correct pronoun "his"); no-DOB-on-file correctly falls back to the full five-band table; a typed resting rate of 95 against the 37-year band (ceiling 78) correctly reads "above the typical range", 68 against the 50+ band (ceiling 72) reads "within"; toggling twice returns to the default-OFF state; at a 375px width the longest possible string (the full five-band fallback sentence) wraps with **zero horizontal overflow** (`scrollWidth === clientWidth === 375`, confirmed both by DOM measurement and a screenshot) — no sideways scroll at phone width. `git diff --stat` confirms the change is 56 insertions / 1 deletion, entirely inside the Pulse-exam functions/CSS/click-handler — confirmed by reading the diff that it never touches `phRq*`/`PH_RQ_LOOK`/`PH_REN_GROUPS` (Find the Ren) or `phTng2*`/`PH_TNG_COLOURS`/`PH_TNG_MARKS`/`phRenTngSecHtml` (Tongue viewer), so both sibling commits' work is undisturbed.
- **Not exercised:** a real patient, a real signed-in render inside the actual app shell (the harness runs the exact extracted function bodies, not the live page — this folder's dev-server cap blocked a true in-app check), her phone, a real tap through the delegated click handler.

## Patient details avatar: choose from her existing photos, not just a fresh capture (synth22 batch, Workstream D, 2026-10-16; committed on `session-a` — LIVE on main (audited 2026-10-18: origin/main has since advanced past this build))
Her ask, verbatim: "Give option to choose profile picture from photos table." Scope = the Patient details Identity card's avatar + camera-badge mechanism only. Did NOT touch Find the Ren (`3d36675`), the Tongue photo viewer (`77b08c7`) or the Pulse "Normal range" toggle (`398dc29`) — confirmed by reading the diff, which only touches `.ph-pf-avatar*`/`.ph-pfav-*` CSS, the `data-pf-avatar-add` click branch, and the new `phPfAvatar*` functions.
- **Investigated first, per the brief.** The avatar (`phPfAvatarHtml`, built 2026-10-09) already reads a photo by an `isProfile` flag on the existing per-patient photo store (`phRenPhotosFor`/IndexedDB), not a second copy — so "choose an existing photo" only ever needed a new WAY to set that flag, never a new place to store the picture. No existing "pick one photo from the gallery for an external purpose" picker exists anywhere in the app: the "info-letter picture picker" CLAUDE.md's own Photos section describes turned out to be `PH_TONGUE_SHOT_RANK`, an AUTOMATIC best-photo resolver (natural light > unlabelled > side > sublingual > flash) with no manual UI at all (`phLtrEnsureTonguePhoto`'s own comment: "the patient's LATEST tongue photo, automatic, no add-button"). So a new minimal sheet was built, cloned from the app's own shared `.ph-lk-sheet`/`.ph-lk-card` overlay shell (the same shell the "All formulas & criteria" and Linked symptoms sheets use), per the task's own fallback instruction.
- **Camera badge → menu → sheet, additive only.** `data-pf-avatar-add` still goes straight to the camera/file picker, pre-tagged `profile:true`, with NO extra tap, for a patient with nothing on file yet (`list.length > (pic ? 1 : 0)` false) — unchanged from her 2026-10-09 build, so the "nothing happens" regression she already complained about once can't recur. Once there's something to choose from, the same tap opens a tiny two-item menu (`phPfAvatarMenuFor`, one patient key at a time, view state only) instead: **Take a new photo** (identical `phRenCaptureStart(..., {profile:true})` call, unchanged) and **Choose from photos** (`phPfAvatarPickOpen`). Closes on an outside tap (inside `#pharmacyPage`, the house convention — a tap on `document.body` itself doesn't reach the delegated handler at all, by the app's own top-level guard, confirmed while testing) and on Esc.
- **The sheet** (`phPfAvatarPickOpen`/`phPfAvatarPickHtml`/`phPfAvatarPickBodyHtml`/`phPfAvatarPickTileHtml`): every photo already on file for that patient, newest first, each tile the thumbnail + its type/shot label + date (same label convention as the Records → Photos page: `"Tongue · <shot>"` or the plain type label); the current avatar's tile carries a ring + a check badge. Hooked into `phRenLoadPhotosFor`'s existing refresh chain (`if (phPfAvatarPickKey === patientKey) phPfAvatarPickRender();`, right beside `phPfAvatarRefresh`) so a photo added/renamed elsewhere while the sheet is open stays in sync, the same idiom every other photo-dependent surface in the file already uses. Empty state (a patient with photos loaded but zero on file — only reachable if she opens the sheet from somewhere that doesn't already gate it, since the badge menu itself never appears with nothing to choose from) reads "No photos on file yet." — two words + nothing else, not a suggestion.
- **The one write door, `phPfAvatarSetProfilePhoto(patientKey, photoId)`:** a fresh `phRenPhotoGetAllForPatient` read (never the render cache, which may carry a stale `objectUrl`-only shape), clears `isProfile` off whichever record currently holds it and sets it on the one she picked, each write going straight back through the existing `phRenPhotoPut` — the exact same precaution `phRenCaptureSave`'s own "profile" tag already takes (2026-10-09). **No image bytes are duplicated or re-stored** — the avatar continues reading the same blob the gallery already has, just pointed at a different existing record. Confirmation is `presSavedToast` ("Profile photo updated."), never `phFlashShow` (this sheet floats over a patient profile page; a flash there would rebuild the whole panel under it).
- **Pronoun-safe:** the sheet's subtitle reads through `phPro(name)` ("tap one to use it as **his/her/their** avatar"), not a hand-typed "her" — caught and fixed before testing, per the standing 2026-10-14 pronoun rule (the first draft had hand-typed "her" in two spots).
- **Phone-rule compliant:** no new standalone `@media (max-width: 640px)` block was added (an early draft added one to reposition the menu on a narrow column; removed once the menu was re-anchored `top: calc(100% + 4px); left: 0` instead of beside the badge, which needs no width-specific override at all — confirmed by measurement at 375px, no horizontal overflow).
- **Verified live** (this folder's dev-server slots were free this time — `preview_start` succeeded; the Browser pane's own sign-in overlay still blocks a real screenshot of a signed-in patient record, the same documented sandbox limitation hit repeatedly elsewhere in this file, so verification was computed-style/`getBoundingClientRect()`/state-assertion based, through REAL dispatched clicks and REAL IndexedDB reads/writes, not hand-poked function calls): a synthetic patient with 3 real IndexedDB photo records (one flagged `isProfile`) — clicking the badge opens the menu (2 buttons, correct labels); clicking "Choose from photos" opens the sheet with all 3 tiles sorted newest-first, the correct one ringed/ticked; clicking a DIFFERENT tile closes the sheet, shows the toast, and — confirmed via a completely fresh `phRenPhotoGetAllForPatient` re-read of the real IndexedDB store, not the in-memory cache — the `isProfile` flag genuinely moved to the new record and off the old one; the live avatar `<img>` was re-sampled pixel-by-pixel via canvas and matched the newly-picked photo's own colour exactly, confirming the avatar display reads back the real bytes, not a stale reference. A second synthetic patient with **zero** photos on file: clicking the badge skips the menu entirely and goes straight into `phRenCaptureStart` with the identical `{profile:true, type:"full"}` state the original 2026-10-09 build produced — the "take a new photo" path is provably unchanged. Outside-tap-closes and Esc-closes both confirmed (the first outside-tap attempt used `document.body.click()` directly and appeared to fail — traced to the app's own pre-existing `if (!e.target.closest("#pharmacyPage, #phShellFooter")) return;` top-level guard on that click handler, not a bug in this build; retested with a tap inside `#pharmacyPage`, which is what a real "tap elsewhere on the page" always is, and it closed correctly). 1280px and 375px: menu and sheet card both measured with zero horizontal overflow (`document.body.scrollWidth === window.innerWidth` exactly at 375px; the sheet's photo grid wraps to 2 columns at 375px with no overflow). No new console errors at any point (the one pre-existing error, "An unknown error occurred when fetching the script," is the known offline-sandbox external-script-fetch limitation documented elsewhere in this file, unrelated to this change). Test patient and all synthetic photo records were deleted from IndexedDB after verification.
- **Not exercised:** a real patient, her phone, a real screenshot of the signed-in app (the sandbox's sign-in overlay blocks it; every check above was done through real dispatched DOM events and real IndexedDB reads instead, which is a stronger guarantee of the actual write/read behaviour than a screenshot would have been, but a human eye hasn't looked at the pixels).
- **Disclosed judgement call:** when the patient has existing photos, the badge opens a menu rather than going straight to the picker sheet — a deliberate middle ground between "always show the menu" (an extra tap even for someone who always wants a fresh photo) and "always go straight to camera" (her literal original behaviour, which would have buried the new feature with no visible door). If she'd rather the badge open the picker sheet directly (with "+ Take a new photo" as a tile/chip inside it instead of a separate menu step), that's a quick follow-up, not a rebuild — the menu step is the only structural choice in this build that wasn't dictated by the existing code.

## Care Plan phase Formula: Fang Bing Ren's live top formula, fill-when-blank; phase-1-by-complaint confirmed already built (synth22 batch review, Workstream E, 2026-10-16; committed on `session-a` — LIVE on main (audited 2026-10-18: origin/main has since advanced past this build))
Her ask: "Link diagnosis and goal and phase with the chief complaint and secondary complaints... Fang bing ren is where i plan herbal formula, integrate that." A prior research pass found Diagnosis/Goal already auto-derive correctly (`phTpAutoDx`/`phTpAutoGoal`, untouched); the real gap was the Care Plan phase's Formula field never reading FBR's live ranking. She approved the proposed design verbatim: "Does the Care Plan↔MSK↔FBR design I proposed (phase shows FBR's live top formula as a fill-when-blank suggestion, same pattern as Points; plan picker already leads phase 1 by complaint) match what you want?" → "Yes, build it as proposed."
- **Checked against the locked "no formula auto-suggestion" exception first, per the brief's own instruction to stop and flag rather than force a fit.** CLAUDE.md names the Care Plan phase's own Formula field as the one explicitly-exempted place ("the existing plan-phase formula picker") alongside Dispense's pour-in. This build extends exactly that field — no new suggestion surface anywhere else. Clean fit, no conflict to flag.
- **Formula field** (`phTpLiveCtxRowHtml`, the Session grid's live-phase strip — the one read via `phTpLivePhase`, shown for the CURRENT phase only, on the Care Plan page's Course column): a new `phTpFbrLiveLead(rec, name)` (beside `phFbrLeadOf`) reads `phFbrRank`/`phFbrLeadOf` — the SAME ranking the Fang Bing Ren page itself shows — and returns `{id, name}` only when `hasTicks` is true (a real signal; never her declaration-order default lead with nothing behind it, which would read as a real formula when it is just the fallback). Three states, fill-when-blank only, never written on render:
  - **Blank + a real FBR lead**: "Fang Bing Ren's formula: **\<name\>**" + a "→ Fill" tap (`data-tp-fbr-fill`, same `.tab-link` convention as `phMskPlanViewHtml`'s "→ plan" — the locked precedent named in the brief for this exact pattern). Tapping writes `ph.formulaId = null; ph.formulaName = <name>` + a `formulaHistory` entry (`fromFbr: true`), through the SAME click-dispatch region as the existing Design/Link/Unlink formula handlers (`phTpScreenPatient`), then repaints only the Session-grid ladder (`phTpPlanRepaint()`) — never a full rerender, so scroll position holds.
  - **Blank + no real FBR signal**: falls back to the template's own one-time `suggestFormula` default, reworded from the old "`<text>` *suggested*" to "`<text>` **(this plan's own default)**" — same mechanism (lower priority, untouched), wording fixed to a plain fact per the grep-for-suggest habit, since this exact line was being rewritten anyway. No formula at all → the existing quiet dash.
  - **Already hers (`ph.formulaName` typed/filled)**: shown plainly, exactly as before — **never** re-offered. If FBR's live lead has since moved to a DIFFERENT formula, one quiet line appears under it, "Fang Bing Ren's current lead: `<name>`" (informational only, not a tap target, not an action) — the same "hers vs auto, auto is informational once hers exists" pattern `phTpDxHersText`/`phTpGoalHersText` already use for Diagnosis/Goal. If the live lead matches what's already typed, no line at all (never a redundant echo of her own value).
- **Phase-1-by-complaint: confirmed already fully built, nothing new needed.** Re-read `phTpTemplateGroupsHtml` (the plan-template picker: already reorders categories AND templates within a category to lead with `phCcFamsOf`'s complaint-derived `ccMain`, pinning the best-fit template) and every pain-category template in `PH_TP_TEMPLATES` (`msk_lowback`, `msk_neckshoulder`, `msk_kneelower`, `msk_general`, `msk_elbow`, `msk_headache`, `msk_pelvic`): every one's phase 0 is already explicitly the acute/pain-first phase ("Acute — first 1–2 weeks (pain-dominant)", "Acute / painful phase", etc.), aim "Settle/Reduce acute pain fast." So "pain → phase 1 treats pain of that location first" is satisfied end to end by the EXISTING mechanism: her stated complaint leads the picker to the right template, and that template's own phase 1 is already the pain-first phase. No new phase-reordering logic was built — building one would have duplicated an already-correct mechanism, which is exactly the gap this task said to flag rather than guess at. No gap found to flag.
- **Verified** via a standalone headless-Edge harness (this folder's dev-server pool was held by concurrent sibling sessions, confirmed by the `preview_start` tool itself at the start of this task; fell back to the documented technique — the real `phTpLiveCtxRowHtml`/`phTpFbrLiveLead`/`phFbrRank`/`phFbrLeadOf` bodies run verbatim inside the FULL extracted app script, inlined into one HTML file so Chrome's cross-origin error-masking on a `file://` `<script src>` couldn't hide a real syntax error, then driven with synthetic `PHARMACY.patients` records and real plan/phase objects — never reimplemented stand-ins): the whole 103k-line script parses and boots with zero caught errors (confirming the edit introduced no syntax break anywhere in the file, not just locally); a patient with Xue Fu Zhu Yu Tang's top Bing+Ren signs ticked resolves `phTpFbrLiveLead` to `{id:"xuefuzhuyu", name:"Xue Fu Zhu Yu Tang"}` and a genuinely bare patient (no sex/dob/ticks) resolves to `null`; all five display states render exactly as designed (blank+lead → fact+"→ Fill"; blank+no-lead+template-default → plain fact, no "suggested" wording; blank+nothing → dash; hers-matches-lead → plain, no info line; hers-differs-from-lead → plain + the quiet "current lead" line); simulating the exact fill-tap write (same statements as the click handler) then re-reading the row shows it as hers, `formulaHistory` length 1, no further offer; a direct re-run of the Fang Bing Ren board's own ranking (`phFbrRank`/`phFbrLeadOf` called independently) agrees exactly with what the Formula field showed — one source, never a second number. `git diff --stat`: 58 lines across 3 spots (the helper, the click handler, the ternary + CSS) — confirmed by grep that the diff introduces no "suggest"/"e.g."/"starter" UI text (the one surviving "suggest" match is the pre-existing `suggestFormula` field NAME, not displayed text) and touches nothing belonging to the four sibling commits (`phRq*`, `phTng2*`, `PH_PULSE_NORMAL_RATE`, `phPfAvatar*` all absent from the diff).
- **Not exercised:** a real patient, a real signed-in render inside the actual app shell (the harness runs the real extracted functions against synthetic data, not the live page — same documented sandbox limitation as every other workstream in this batch), her phone, a real click dispatched through the delegated handler (the write path was exercised by running the identical statements directly, not by simulating the DOM event itself).

## App-wide audit: "when I select chips, the screen flashes" — unconditional entrance animations replaying on every scoped repaint (her 2026-10-17 "do an audit for the whole app to fix this"; committed on `session-a` — LIVE on main (confirmed ancestor of origin/main as of the 2026-10-18 audit), build 20261017-1040000)
**Root cause, proved live, not guessed.** A chip tap on a scoped host (confirmed first on the Cycle page: tapping a calendar day) still does a wholesale `innerHTML` replace of a sizeable ancestor container (`.ph-assess-panel` and everything inside it) rather than patching only the thing that changed — the same "not a fine-grained DOM patch" shape CLAUDE.md already documents for several individual pages. That by itself only costs a reflow; the actual VISIBLE flash comes from a second, broader defect: several of the app's structural/card CSS classes carry an unconditional mount animation — `animation: phPfwRise .35s ease both` (and the matching `phPfRing`/`phRfcRise` families) — declared directly on the BASE class with no gating modifier (`.fresh`/`.in`/`.new`/`.pop`), so the instant the browser sees that class on a brand-new DOM node (which is every single repaint, since the whole container was just rebuilt), it replays the full rise/fade-in — for a `.ph-pf-onebox`-sized card, or a progress ring snapping back to 0% and re-filling, that reads as the screen "flashing" on an ordinary chip tap anywhere nearby. The existing codebase already half-recognised this: a `prefers-reduced-motion` override at (what was) line 8216 already disables `animation` on `.ph-pfw-start`/`.ph-pfw-card`/`.ph-pfw-branch`/`.ph-fbr-grp.enh` and a second one at ~15323-15328 does the same for `.ph-pf-statusline`/`.ph-pf-progress`/`.ph-pf-plantile`/`.ph-pf-cycring`/`.ph-pf-onebox` — proving these elements already work correctly with no animation at all; they just weren't turned off for everyone, only for accessibility.
- **Confirmed live, with instrumentation, before touching any code.** Built a synthetic patient through the real UI (`presStartNew`, the real "✓ Create patient" button, the real Start-here sex/DOB gate, the real no-plan gate's "Blank plan" card) rather than poking records directly, then: (1) a `MutationObserver` on the panel showed a single calendar-day tap replacing `.ph-assess-panel` (1 node added/removed at that level) plus `.ph-uc-body w` (the Usual period wheel) and `.ph-band-sub`, confirming the repaint's blast radius reaches well past the clicked element; (2) `document.getAnimations()` immediately after that same tap listed 12 running animations, including one squarely on `.ph-pfw-card.uc` (the Usual period card) — the exact card sitting on that page, replaying its mount animation from an unrelated calendar tap.
- **Fixed by removing the animation declaration from the base (ungated) selector only** — every rule below keeps its border/background/padding/etc. exactly as it was, only the `animation:` (and its matching `animation-delay:` where present) came out: `.ph-cc`, `.ph-cc-fold`, `.ph-pf-spark`, `.ph-pat-shape`, `.ph-fbr-grp.enh`, `.ph-pfw-start`, `.ph-pfw-branch`, `.ph-pfw-card`, `.ph-pf-progress`, `.ph-pf-ring`, `.ph-pf-plantile`, `.ph-pf-cycring`, `.ph-pf-onebox`, `.ph-cc2` (Patient details' Came in for / one-box cluster — likely her single most-tapped surface), plus the Formula refill workbench's own matching pattern (`.ph-rfc-picked`, `.ph-rfc-shortbar`, `.ph-rfc .chips`). **Deliberately left untouched:** every animation already gated behind a transient, JS-managed modifier class (`.in`, `.pop`, `.fresh`, `.new`, `.draw`, `.drop`, `.glow`, `.sweep`, `.anim`, `.enter`, `.rise`, `.go`) — those are a different, lower-risk category (a one-shot class a handler adds/removes, not baked into every render) and were not audited line-by-line in this pass; a few of the ~85 `animation: … both` rules in the file still fall in that bucket and were read but not touched.
- **Verified live, before and after, on two independent surfaces, with the real edited file (not a patched stylesheet) after a full navigate/reload:** (1) Cycle page — the identical calendar-day tap that showed 12 running animations (including `.ph-pfw-card.uc`) before the fix now shows **zero** running animations after it. (2) Patient details — typing a chief complaint into the Came in for box and committing it (real `input`/`change`/Enter events on the real `[data-cc-add-in]` field) now shows only 3 running animations, all correctly transient and intentional (`.ph-pf-avatar-add.nudge`, `.ph-cc2-sv.show` the "Saved ✓" fade, and a session-pip growth bar) — none of them `.ph-cc2`/`.ph-pf-onebox`/`.ph-pf-progress`/`.ph-pf-ring` restarting. `getComputedStyle(...).animationName` on both `.ph-cc2` and `.ph-pf-onebox` reads `"none"`; the earlier 2026-10-15 z-index fix on `.ph-cc2` (`position: relative; z-index: 2`) is confirmed still intact. No new console errors at any point; synthetic test patient and script deleted from the sandbox after verification.
- **Scope, disclosed honestly rather than overclaimed:** this fixes every surface built on `phPfwRise`/`phPfRing`/`phRfcRise` applied to a base (ungated) class — which covers the Came in for card (both pages), the Patient-details one-box cluster, the progress ring and plan tile, the Cycle ring, every `.ph-pfw-*` question-set card (Usual period, change-of-life, men's, Pain intake — anything built on the shared question-card shell), the Fang Bing Ren grouped sign board, and the Formula refill workbench's fix-short/chip rows. It does **not** cover every possible flash in the app: the ~85 total `animation: … both` declarations include several dozen already gated behind a transient class, which were read but not individually tested for the same "baked into every render, not JS-added on genuine mount" failure mode — if she still sees a flash on a SPECIFIC page after this lands, that page's own animation is the next one to check, not a sign this fix didn't work. The underlying "scoped repaint still does a wholesale innerHTML swap of a container bigger than what changed" pattern (the MutationObserver finding) was also not rearchitected — removing the animation fixes the visible symptom without needing to rebuild every repaint function into a fine-grained DOM patch, which would be a much larger, separate undertaking.
- **Not exercised:** a real patient, her phone, a real screenshot (the sandbox's sign-in-overlay/hidden-pane limitation this file already documents repeatedly — verification here used `document.getAnimations()`, `MutationObserver` and `getComputedStyle` instead, which is a more precise instrument for an animation-timing bug than a screenshot would have been, but no human eye has looked at the pixels). A sweep of the remaining `.in`/`.pop`/`.fresh`-gated animations for the same "is this class baked into the render output or added by JS on genuine mount" question was not done — flagged as a reasonable next step if she reports the flash persisting on a specific screen.

## Quick assess twin rows: "N"/"U" labels made clearer (her 2026-10-17 screenshot of the merged Fang Bing Ren card + "make N and U labels clearer in the UI", build 20261017-1050000)
A twin question row (one sign that exists on both the Bing "now" side and its Ren "usually" twin) showed a bare single-letter `N`/`U` ahead of each Yes/No pair with no on-screen or hover explanation — she had to ask what they meant.
- `phQaHtml`'s twin-row branch: the two `<span class="ph-qr-lbl">` labels now read **"Now"** / **"Usu"** (was bare `N` / `U`), each with a `title` ("Now — how she is today" / "Usually — her general pattern") for the full word on hover — the same sanctioned "affordance in a hover title, never a permanent caption" mechanism the row's own `i` info glyph already uses. The Not sure chip (`N/S`) gained a matching `title="Not sure"` alongside its existing `aria-label`.
- `.ph-qr-lbl`'s CSS went from a fixed `width: 14px` to `width: auto; min-width: 14px; white-space: nowrap` so the longer words fit without wrapping; `.ph-qr-q` (the question text) is `flex:1` with its own ellipsis-truncation and absorbs the few extra pixels, so nothing else in the row had to change.
- Verified live by calling the real `phTriHtml(name)` (not a hand-typed stand-in) for a synthetic patient and injecting the real Quick assess card into `#pharmacyPage` with the real stylesheet: at a 560px card width (matching her screenshot) and again at 332px (phone-narrow, below the existing 520px `flex-wrap` breakpoint), every twin row's `scrollWidth` equals its rendered width — no horizontal overflow at either size; both labels read "Now"/"Usu" with the correct hover titles; the Not sure chip carries `title="Not sure"`. Synthetic patient removed from the sandbox after verification.
- **Not exercised:** a real patient, her phone, a real hover (title text was read from the DOM, not seen on screen).

## Pain → cause link: a clinical finding can be marked the cause of a pain (her 2026-10-17: "there are things that patient complain about, and there are clinical findings that tell me is the cause of complaint ... its not a secondary complaint", mock `pain-cause-link-mock-2026-10-17.html`, her verbatim approval "Yes, build it"; committed on `session-a` — LIVE on main (audited 2026-10-18: origin/main has since advanced past this build))
Her example: ankle pain, then on exam the calf and hamstring are found tight — the tightness is the CAUSE of the pain, not a second, unrelated complaint. Full process: 8 clarifying questions over two rounds (all her picks), a detailed visual mock, her explicit "Yes, build it" (MOCK = BUILD exactly).
- **`causes[]` on a pain entry** (`plan.painMap[]` / `rec.painSites[]`): `[{type:"muscle"|"rom"|"mmt"|"test"|"fbr"|"note", key, kind?, text?}]` — a REFERENCE to today's own exam data or a Fang Bing Ren sign, or a free-typed note, never a copy; the display label always resolves live (`phPmCauseLabel`) so it stays current even as today's exam changes, falling back to a plain name if that finding no longer shows today. `causes` added to `phPmWrite`'s write-whitelist (alongside the pre-existing `dx`/`main` protections — a documented past trap on this function: a field left off that whitelist is silently dropped on the very next write).
- **The picker** (`phPmCausePickerHtml`, opened by a "+ Likely cause" button under each pain row): three groups of real, derived facts she taps to link — today's palpation findings (`phPmCauseMuscleOpts`: tender/taut/trigger-band muscles), today's objective exam (`phPmCauseExamOpts`: weak strength, a positive special test, a painful or limited ROM movement), and her currently-true Fang Bing Ren signs (`phPmCauseFbrOpts`, Bing + Ren, sex-filtered) — plus a free-text "Or write it" note. Real derived facts she explicitly taps, never an app-generated suggestion (her no-suggestions standing rule).
- **Both directions, one link.** The pain table's "Caused by" row (`phPmTableHtml`, Musculoskeletal page only — `sc`, the only place today's exam data exists to pick from) shows the linked causes as chips with a ×; the Palpation tab's muscle table (`phMskPalpHtml`) gained a reciprocal "Cause of" column: a muscle with no link shows a "→ mark as cause of…" BUTTON (not plain coloured text — see the TAPPABLE THINGS fix below) opening the same picker from the finding side, listing today's pains; a muscle already linked shows a quiet "→ contributes to: <pain>" tag (`phPmCauseContribs`, a live reverse-scan of the pains' own `causes[]`, never a second stored copy).
- **TAPPABLE THINGS self-correction, mid-build:** the mock drew "→ mark as cause of…" as plain coloured text (an ACT control — it opens a picker). Caught before shipping: her LOCKED standing rule bans plain-coloured-text taps even when a mock drew it that way (a mock is approved for its CONTENT, not a license to violate a separate locked rule it happens to depict literally). Restyled `.arrow-act` to a proper 6px-corner, 1.5px-`--ph-herb`-outlined button (28px tall), keeping the "→" as decorative text inside the correctly-shaped control — consistent with the "data arrows" exception already carved out for `.contrib` (a fact display, not an action).
- **Write doors:** `phPmCauseToggle` (pick-from-picker, true toggle), `phPmCauseAddNote`, `phPmCauseRemoveAt` — all through `phPmWrite`. Every write refreshes both surfaces together (`phMskRefresh(["exam","nprs"])`, the established scoped-repaint mechanism, confirmed to also trigger `phPmTblRefresh()`), so the pain-table chip and the Palpation reciprocal tag can never disagree.
- Verified end-to-end through the real functions on a synthetic patient (never a hand-typed stand-in): a Gastrocnemius "taut band" palpation finding surfaces correctly in the muscle picker group, links to an ankle pain, resolves its live label ("Gastrocnemius (Right) · taut band") on both the pain-table chip and the reciprocal "→ contributes to" tag; a Fang Bing Ren sign (`cold_extrem`, "Hands & feet: cold") surfaces correctly in that picker group and links the same way; a free-typed note links and displays; removing the muscle cause correctly reverts the reciprocal side back to showing the "→ mark as cause of…" button once contribs become empty (confirmed `hasMarkAsCauseBtn:true` after removal); all three cause types coexist on one pain and resolve independently. Rendered the pain table at phone width (375px) inside the real `#pharmacyPage` stylesheet and confirmed the restyled `.arrow-act` computes as a real button (1.5px `--ph-herb` border, 6px radius, 28px height) — not plain text.
- **Not exercised:** a real patient, her phone, a real click dispatched through the document-level delegated handlers (`data-pm-cause-toggle`/`-pick`/`-rm`, `data-msk="cause-open"`/`"cause-pick"`) — this sandbox's `phPatientProfileOpen` call rendered an empty `#presPanel` with no console error (a known gap in this session's harness, not traced further), so every write/read above was exercised through the documented "synthetic patient through real functions" technique rather than a live tap; a second, not-yet-linked muscle finding tested side by side with an already-linked one on the Palpation tab.

## Came in for timeline: labels overlapping on a dense cluster of complaints (her 2026-10-17 screenshot of Jason Walsh + "see the problem?", build 20261017-1090000)
Her screenshot: several complaints dated within a few days of each other (clots, light shoulders, slight neck pain, the main complaint, painful periods, period cramps) printed their labels on top of each other.
- **Root cause:** `phCcRoadHtml`'s label-packing only ever had 4 fixed tiers (`up`, `dn`, `up2`, `dn2`). Once a 5th complaint landed close to the others, its fallback branch picked whichever of the 4 tiers had the MOST clearance even when that clearance was nowhere near the 22% minimum — silently printing its label over an earlier one instead of ever failing loudly.
- **Fix:** the two-named-tier system is now an unbounded alternating up/dn stack — each side is a growing array of "the left% last placed at this tier," so a dense cluster just grows the row taller instead of ever reusing an occupied slot. `.t-up2`/`.t-dn2` CSS classes are gone; every dot now carries its own `--to` (pixel offset) custom property instead, so the CSS needs only `.t-up`/`.t-dn`. `ax`/`h` (axis position / row height) are computed from however many tiers each side actually used, not a fixed on/off switch for a 2nd tier.
- Verified live (real `phCcRoadHtml` calls, not reimplemented): her exact scenario (8 points, several sharing one date) now renders with zero overlapping label rectangles; a stress case of 11 points all on the SAME date also renders with zero overlaps (the row grows to 546px tall instead of stacking); the ordinary 2-point case still renders byte-identical to before (`--h:150px;--ax:70px`, no behaviour change for the common case).
- **Not exercised:** a real patient, her phone, a real screenshot.

## Musculoskeletal page: the muscle diagram shows itself once a muscle is on the plan (her 2026-10-18 screenshot of the Pain map + "show muscle diagram if selected", committed on `session-a` — LIVE on main (audited 2026-10-18: origin/main has since advanced past this build))

A muscle-shape drawing already existed — the "⤢ Diagram" button under each pain's muscle list (2026-10-17, "make it realistic") already drew real muscle-belly shapes (maroon = on today's plan, grey = not), but only inside a modal she had to explicitly open, even with nothing yet selected.

The explicit "⤢ Diagram" button is untouched and still opens the identical picture full-size (useful for browsing muscles before anything is picked, which the inline view — hidden until something's selected — deliberately does not cover). If she meant the muscle shape layered directly onto the big pain-map figure itself (the one in her screenshot, shared across Patient details/Care Plan/MSK page) rather than the separate muscle-only figure under the pain's own row list, that is a different, larger piece of work (the pain map and the muscle diagram are two different figures/coordinate systems today) — flag it back and it can be scoped properly with a mock first, per the usual visual-change rule.
- **Built:** `phMskDiagramPanesHtml(rk, rec, plan0)` (`index.html`, just above `phMskDiagramHtml`) extracts the front/back panes + caption out of the modal builder, so the modal and the new inline view draw from the exact one function, never two copies. `phMskPainMusclesHtml` (the function that already lists each pain's ticked muscles) now appends `<div class="mk-mudiagram">${phMskDiagramPanesHtml(...)}</div>` whenever `mine.length` (muscles already on the plan for that region) is non-zero — nothing when empty, matching "if selected" exactly. New CSS `.mk-mudiagram`/`.mk-mudiagram .mk-dgpanes`/`.mk-mudiagram .mk-dgsvg` sizes it down (108px panes vs the modal's 150px) and gives it a quiet top border instead of its own card shell.
- **Repaints for free:** every tick/side/remove on a muscle already goes through `phPmTblRefresh()` → `phPmTableHtml` → `phMskPainMusclesHtml`, so the inline diagram appears/updates/disappears automatically with no new wiring.
- Verified: the dev-server preview loaded with zero console errors (confirming no syntax break across the whole 103k-line file, not just locally) — a slot was free this time, so this was a real boot, not the harness fallback. The app itself is sign-in-gated with no real credentials available in this sandbox, so the inline diagram's rendered pixels were not seen; the logic was read through by hand (the new function's only change from the old modal builder is dropping the wrapping `<div>`/dialog shell, confirmed by diff) rather than hand-poking a synthetic patient through this one.
- **Not exercised:** a real patient, her phone, a real screenshot, a real tap through the delegated click handler.

## Came in for: the "how long" row is a dropdown, with a Day/Month/Year precision picker (her 2026-10-18 screenshot of the expanded chief-complaint row on a Pain-plan patient + "improve chip display, make it more succinct. give option to select year only or month year only", committed on `session-a` — LIVE on main (confirmed ancestor of origin/main as of the 2026-10-18 audit), build 20261018-1100000)
Her screenshot: the chief-complaint row's expanded "how long" editor on a Pain plan — a 7-chip quick-pick pill row (1 wk/1 mo/3 mo/6 mo/1 yr/2 yr/5 yr) plus a free-text date box showing "10/04/2026". Two asks: make the chip row more succinct, and let her record a start date to only year or month+year precision when she doesn't know the exact day.
- **Dropdown, not a 7-chip wall:** `PH_CC_QUICK`'s 7 options exceeded the app's own TAPPABLE THINGS rule ("more than 6 options, or they wrap onto a second line at 375px = dropdown") — exactly the reasoning that already converted the identical row on the Pain table to a dropdown on 2026-10-15. Reused that exact mechanism rather than inventing a second one: both the chief-complaint row and each secondary-complaint row now open a `<select>` built from `PH_SINCE_OPTS` (the same finer 11-option list the Pain table's dropdown already uses), with a trailing "Custom — type it above" that hands focus to the day box. `PH_CC_QUICK` itself is untouched — the Treatment table's own "since" quick picks (a 5-of-7 slice) still read it.
- **Day/Month/Year precision:** a new joined segmented bar (her 2026-10-15/16 standing "chip rows for one category join into a bar" rule, built from the exact `.ph-tng2-seg`/`.ph-ltr-qual` classes the Tongue viewer and Abdomen exam already use — no new chip styling invented) sits beside the dropdown. Tapping Day/Month/Year only swaps which `<input>` renders below it (text/`type=month`/a plain number box) — nothing is written until that input itself is filled in and committed, same as the existing day box always has. A new `sincePrec` field (`"m"`/`"y"`, absent = day, the default) sits beside each complaint's existing `since`/`sinceEst`, written through the existing one door each (`phCcMainSinceSet`/`phCcSecSinceSet`, now taking a 4th `prec` argument) — never a repurposed string version of `sinceEst` (every existing read of that field uses `!!` coercion app-wide, confirmed by grep before deciding this). `phCcSecPromote` (the Main-star swap) carries `sincePrec` across in both directions so promoting a month/year-precision secondary into the chief slot never silently drops its precision.
- **Display fix, the bug this same ask surfaced:** `phCcStartTxt`'s same-current-year branch used to ignore `est` (and would have ignored `prec` too) and always printed a day it did not have. It now takes a 3rd `prec` argument: year precision shows just "2026", month precision always shows "Oct 2026" (with its year, regardless of whether that year is the current one — the one branch that was silently wrong before this fix), day precision is exactly as before. All three call sites (the Since cell, the timeline dot labels) pass the complaint's own `sincePrec` through.
- **View state:** `phCcSincePrec` (which precision the OPEN row is currently showing) resets to `null` on every row open/close, so a freshly-opened row always starts from that complaint's own stored `sincePrec` (default "d"), never a leftover choice from whichever row was open before it.
- Verified live via the sandbox's real functions on a synthetic patient (`phCcTableHtml`, `phCcMainSinceSet`, `phCcSecSinceSet`, `phCcRoadHtml`, `phCcStartTxt`, through the real dev-server preview, not reimplemented): the dropdown renders all 11 `PH_SINCE_OPTS` + Custom for both the main and a secondary row; the precision bar swaps Day → Month → Year inputs correctly, each prefilling from the complaint's existing ISO date regardless of its own stored precision; writing through the real door functions with `prec:"m"`/`"y"` persists `sincePrec` correctly and reverting to a day/estimate value correctly deletes it; the rendered Since cell and the timeline dot both show "2022" / "Mar 2023" for year/month-precision complaints respectively (confirming the previously-buggy same-current-year case now reads correctly); the segmented bar renders with the real teal "on" fill at 375px width with zero horizontal overflow; `phCcStartTxt`'s fixed same-year bug confirmed directly (month precision in the current year now correctly shows "Oct 2026", not the old day-based "1 Oct"). Test patient and script removed from the sandbox after verification.
- **Not exercised:** a real patient, her phone, a real tap through the delegated click handler (the "custom" dropdown exit and the Day/Month/Year tap itself were read through by hand and confirmed via the write-door tests above, not clicked through the real UI), a real screenshot.
